← Symptom definition

PEM / PEM-like state

Calendar records · 1486

Profoundly disabling · Practitioner · 2026-11-20
Supporting recordExertional and sensory intolerance

Physical, cognitive and sensory activity can precipitate tingling in the forearms/legs, nape pressure, cognitive slowing, marked malaise and involuntary sleep. The patient distinguishes this from ordinary muscle fatigue. Evening attacks and repeated waves over days are described. Meals often relieved milder episodes rapidly, with exceptions.

CTX-008 · S3:13-28 · S2:9016-9032

Occurrence · Trigger-linked episodes
Reported triggers · Patient-reported physical, cognitive and sensory exertion; some episodes follow sleep loss, emotional effort, or treatment changes. Other episodes have no established trigger.

Basis
Patient-described spectrum, not a proven single mechanism. Crashy = vulnerable/warning phase, potentially aborted. Poisoned and malaise = experiential qualities. A crash is a distinct severe endpoint, not obligatory. Deep internal tingling/pulsing and sensory intolerance remain independently recorded baseline features that often intensify with episodes. Individual observations do not establish causation or an exertional trigger.
context_cards:CTX-R21-SYMPTOM-REVIEW; historical_entries:S3:symptom:013; context_cards:CTX-008; archive_messages:S1-M22525

  • May include · Occipital pain during the PEM warning phase
    Basis
    Occipital/nape pain can occur in a reported warning phase but is not present in every episode.
    historical_entries:S3:symptom:013
  • May progress to · Crash
    Basis
    Explicit severe crash, sometimes with CNS fatigue or sleep pressure. Many PEM-like/warning states do not progress to a full crash.
    diary_observations:S2-OBS-8064; historical_entries:S3:symptom:013
  • Often intensifies during · Deep internal tingling / pulsing
    Basis
    Baseline deep internal tingling/pulsing may be present between episodes, with reported escalation during PEM-like states. Keep a separate symptom identity.
    historical_entries:S3:symptom:013; diary_observations:S2-OBS-9103
  • Often intensifies during · Light intolerance
    Basis
    Light intolerance exists at baseline and can become extreme during PEM/crashes. Bright/white light can provoke a described spinal shivering response.
    historical_entries:S3:symptom:023; diary_observations:S2-OBS-3064
  • Often intensifies during · Sound intolerance
    Basis
    Sound intolerance exists at baseline and can intensify with PEM/crashes; patient describes sharp head pain from noise.
    historical_entries:S3:symptom:023; diary_observations:S2-OBS-1065
  • terminology_overlap · Evening-type sensory waves / tingling
    Basis
    The recurring evening-type pulsing/tingling is tracked separately because it can occur without an exertion-linked crash.
    diary_observations:S2-OBS-6312; S2-OBS-7916; archive_messages:S1-M72650
